                                BACKGROUND

Robert Schneider working with contact Valentina Fromm was able to gain copy of
the following information, specifically aimed at the Beresan District German
persons.

Valentina selected people in the files held in the Security Service Archives of
the Ukraine in Nikolaev Region, picking information on the German names she
found there.  These Archives are now in Operative Information Board of Ukrainian
Internal Affairs of Nikolaev Region.  There are more than 7,000 personal records
of Germans held.

Before 1991, these lists were highly classified and not available to anyone.
Since the Ukraine became independent in 1991, permission to publish such
information has existed.

Valentina, had Oleg translate the list into a computer file and then this file
was provided back to Robert for sharing with the Beresan District German
researchers.

The dates of arrests are given in the lists, you can see the v name, place of
residence, date of arrest, execution and rehabilitation. The names of the
districts are Soviet, but the old names are given opposite every surname

                   NOTE
These District names were used for the rayons from 1922-1938.
    Karl-Liebnecht is the Beresan
    Spartakus is the Liebental
    Friedrich Engels is the Kutschurgan
*************************************************************************

While preparing this information for posting here on the Web, we took some notes
to try to answer some obvious questions folks will have as they use this
information.

First we note that the name of the individual involved is in the "Patronymic"
form - which provides the father's given name as if it were a middle name.  So,
when you see Jakob Jakob or the like, read the 2nd name as the father's name
rather than a middle name the same as the first.

The use of the Patronymic system of names is something we who use this
information will have to keep in mind each time we view an entry!

Then we see the use of terms "sentenced to destruction", "destroyed", and
"rehabilitated".

We think if you use the thought that destruction and destroyed equates to death,
that you will be able to better understand the meaning of this data.

The term of "rehabilitated" was a process that was undertaken to clear the
person's name of having performed an act against the government.

Germans were rehabilitated if they didn't have any crime, because the article
154/158 of the Criminal Code of the Ukrainia Scocialist Republik (the former
USSR) was abrogated.

We think the term "v." can be read clearly as if it were in German, meaning
"von" or from.

Also, it appears that sentences that reflect "s" equates to the word years.
Example 8 s would be 8 years or 10 s would equate to 10 years.

It is worthy to note that some of the entries appear to reflect an open file
rather than a conclusion of being rehabilitated.

At this time, we do not have meanings we can share for "three tuple", but it
appears that it obviously was some type of legal trial to determine or confirm
the guilt of a charged individual.

As for the terms of "NKVD" "UNKVD" "USSR" "GPU" "UkSSR" and similar, we are not
going to attempt to explain them here.

INDEX - German Name And finally - as we prepared this file, we came to the
conclusion that we needed to provide an index with the German names.

You will find that index at the end of the listing.  You will note that we were
not able to give you a 100% list of "German" view of the names, but we did the
best we could, and hope that it aids the Beresan District researcher.


************************************************************************
DATA AS PROVIDED FROM RECORDS___________________________________________

1. Aman Christian Philipovich, 1876, german, from peasants, formation an
average, lived at v. Velikovelidarovzy Region of Elanetz. Collective farmer.
Arrested 01.12.1937. Deciding a NKVD USSR and USSR Public prosecutor 02.01.1938.
sentenced to destruction. Destroyed 17.01.1938. Rehabilitated 1963.

2. Bast Adam Ivanovich, 1874, v. Leonopoll Region of New-Odessa, german, from
peasants, formation initial, lived at v. Neudorf. Guardian in collective farm.
Three-tuple under UNKVD in Nikolaev area sentenced to destruction and forfeiture
of property. Destroyed 01.06.1938. Rehabilitated 1989.

3. Bast Philip Ivanovich, 1885, v. Velikodarovka Region of Elanetz, german, from
peasants, formation initial, lived at v. Neudorf. Manager by farm of collective
farm of name a Rosa Luxembourg. Three-tuple under UNKVD in Nikolaev area
23.03.1938. sentenced to destruction. Destroyed 25.09.1938. Rehabilitated 1957.

4. Bast Rafail Alfonsovich, 1909, v. Neudorf Region of Elanetz, german, from

to destruction and forfeiture of property. Destroyed 01.06.1938. The items of
information on rehabilitation are absent.
